AY2027 Admission Guide: Doctoral Program
Graduate School of Information Science and Technology
The University of Tokyo
Examinations Conducted in AY2026
*In the event that the contents of this Admission Guide are revised, the latest information is to be posted on the
admission webpage of this graduate school (https://www.i.u-tokyo.ac.jp/edu/entra/entra_e.shtml). Applicants
should periodically check the webpage.
Education and Research Goals
The education and research goals at this graduate school are to provide an environment where students can
master their systematic knowledge of information science and technology, and to foster individuals who will
act as leaders in research and development through the pursuit of the scientific method, thereby contributing to
the further development of this field.
Prospective Students
Those with deep insight into information science and technology, who have the necessary abilities for
international activities, and who possess strong motivation and talent for pioneering uncharted fields to create
the future.
Admission Policy
1. The doctoral program of the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology, the University of
Tokyo, seeks students who have sufficient technical knowledge of specialized topics and have a strong desire
to contribute to society through research in their specialized fields. Students are expected to develop a
distinguished expanse of knowledge in the field of information science and technology.
2. During the screening process, we will evaluate comprehensively if applicants meet the following conditions,
through submitted documents, written examinations, oral examinations, etc.:
(a) Applicants have a deep knowledge of the specialized topics they wish to study and have the ability to
contribute to the development of academic fields through research on information science and technology.
(b) Applicants can set their own advanced research goals based on the needs of society and achieve those goals
by utilizing fundamental knowledge of information science and technology.
(c) Applicants have enough language capabilities to compete on a global stage and credentials to contribute to
the improvement of future society.
1. Entrance Examinations and Entrance Date
Examinations are held twice a year, Summer Entrance Examinations and Winter Entrance Examinations, in
each department: Computer Science; Mathematical Informatics; Information Physics and Computing;
1
The document is a translation from the official Japanese version.
In the case of conflict, the Japanese version shall prevail and be conclusive.
Information and Communication Engineering; Mechano-Informatics; and Creative Informatics.
The entrance dates for successful applicants for the Summer Entrance Examinations and for the Winter Entrance
Examinations are in April 2027 and October 2027 respectively (hereafter referred to as “Standard Entrance
Dates”). Some departments may allow October 2026 entry for successful applicants for the Summer Entrance
Examinations and may allow April 2027 entry for successful applicants for the Winter Entrance Examinations
(hereafter referred to as “Early Entrance”) if applicants indicate this preference on the application form. If
applicants are required to newly apply for a Certificate of Eligibility for a Status of Residence (COE) to enter
the University of Tokyo, the Standard Entrance Dates are highly recommended.* The Standard Entrance Dates
are also recommended for other applicants, as a Letter of Acceptance for Admission may be issued later than
the time specified in Section 8 below due to screening by The University of Tokyo Security Export Control, etc.
*As described in 10. Important Notes (6), applicants with foreign nationalities must obtain a visa permitting enrollment at
a Japanese graduate school by the time of enrollment procedures. Since obtaining a new visa normally takes approximately
three months after the admission notification for successful applicants, a visa cannot be obtained in time for Early Entrance.
Regarding the details on the procedure from announcement of the examination result to enrollment, refer to the
accompanying document “Timeline of Enrollment Procedures for New International Students”.
2. Requirements for Eligibility
The eligibility requirements for applicants desiring April 2027 entry are as follows. If desiring October 2026
entry, replace “March 31, 2027” below with “September 30, 2026”, and if desiring October 2027 entry, replace
“March 31, 2027” with “September 30, 2027”.
(1) Those who have obtained, or are expected to obtain by March 31, 2027, a Master’s or Professional degree
at a Japanese university.*1
(2) Those who have obtained or are expected to obtain a Master’s degree by March 31, 2027, from the National
Institution for Academic Degrees and Quality Enhancement of Higher Education (NIAD-QE).
(3) Those who have obtained, or are expected to obtain by March 31, 2027, a degree equivalent to a Master’s
or Professional degree in a foreign country.*2
(4) Those who have completed, or are expected to complete a program at an educational institution in Japan that
is part of the school education system of a foreign country and has a graduate school program of that foreign
country, and that is designated so by the Minister of Education, Culture, Sports, Science and Technology,
and consequently have obtained or are expected to obtain by March 31, 2027, a degree equivalent to a
Master’s or Professional degree.
(5) Those who have completed or are expected to complete a program at the United Nations University and
have obtained or are expected to obtain a degree equivalent to a Master’s degree by March 31, 2027.
(6) Those who have passed or are expected to pass by March 31, 2027, a screening for the fundamental ability
for doctorate dissertation research as specified in Article 16-2 of the Standards for Establishment of Graduate
Schools at a school abroad, an educational institution mentioned in Requirement (4) above, or the United
Nations University, and are recognized by this graduate school as having academic abilities at least
equivalent to a holder of a Master's degree.*3
2
The document is a translation from the official Japanese version.
In the case of conflict, the Japanese version shall prevail and be conclusive.
(7) Those who have graduated from a Japanese university or have completed at least 16 years of academic
education overseas, and have been engaged in research at a university, research institute or other institution
in Japan or abroad for at least 2 years, or are expected to have been engaged in research for at least 2 years
by March 31, 2027, and are recognized by this graduate school as having academic abilities at least
equivalent to a holder of a Master’s or Professional degree as a result of the research.*1*2*3
(8) Those aged twenty-four (24) years or older as of March 31, 2027, whom the Graduate School of Information
Science and Technology recognizes as having academic abilities at least equivalent to a holder of a Master’s
or Professional degree, based on individual screening of entrance qualification.*4
*1 Japanese universities mentioned in Requirements (1) and (7) above are universities within the boundaries of Japan and
as prescribed in Article 83 of the School Education Law.
*2 Requirements (3) and (7) above include the case of having completed a correspondence program provided by a foreign
school of said country while residing in Japan.
*3 1) Those who wish to apply based on Requirements (6) or (7) above must submit the documents designated by the
Graduate School of Information Science and Technology to the Admissions Office (ref. Section 11 below) by
Wednesday, May 13, 2026, in the case of Summer Entrance Examinations, and by Monday, October 26, 2026, in the
case of Winter Entrance Examinations, as the eligibility for application is confirmed individually in advance by
document screening. Applicants should inquire in advance at the Graduate School of Information Science and
Technology Admissions Office for information about documents to be submitted.
2) Those who are recognized as having academic abilities that are at least equivalent to a holder of a Master’s or
Professional degree through screening of entrance qualifications will be permitted to apply for and take the entrance
examination.
*4 1) Those covered under Requirement (8) above are those who are not covered under (1) through (7) above and whom
this graduate school has recognized, through individual screening of entrance qualifications, as having academic
abilities at least equivalent to a holder of a Master’s or Professional degree.
2) Those who wish to apply based on Requirement (8) above must submit the documents designated by the Graduate
School of Information Science and Technology to the Admissions Office (ref. Section 11 below) by Wednesday, May
13, 2026, in the case of Summer Entrance Examinations, and by Monday, October 26, 2026, in the case of Winter
Entrance Examinations, as the eligibility for application is confirmed individually in advance by document screening.
Applicants should inquire in advance at the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology Admissions
Office regarding the documents to be submitted.
3) Those who are recognized, through screening of entrance qualification, as having academic abilities at least
equivalent to a holder of a Master's or Professional degree will be permitted to apply for and take the entrance
examination.
3. Screening Process
Screening of the successful applicants for admission will be conducted by comprehensively taking into account
the results of the written examination, oral examination, submitted documents, and the applicant's Master's
thesis or its alternative.
(Note 1) The details of the screening process may differ for each department. Please refer to the Admission Guide for the
applicable department for detailed information.
(Note 2) As professional applicants may be eligible to apply for a Doctoral Program [Special Selection for Professionals],
they should also obtain the Admission Guide of Doctoral Program [Special Selection for Professionals] and refer
to the Admission Guide for the applicable department for detailed information.
3
The document is a translation from the official Japanese version.
In the case of conflict, the Japanese version shall prevail and be conclusive.
4. Examination Schedules and Locations
(1) Summer Entrance Examinations will be held during the period from Monday, August 24 through Tuesday,
September 1, 2026. For details of the examinations, refer to the Admission Guide for each department. The
examination schedule for those who are expected to complete the Master’s program of this graduate school
may differ from the above schedule depending on the department; follow the Admission Guide of each
department.
(2) Winter Entrance Examinations will be held between late January and mid-February 2027. For details of the
examinations, refer to the Admission Guide of each department. The examination schedule for those who
are expected to complete the Master’s program of this graduate school may differ from the above schedule
depending on the department; follow the Admission Guide of each department.

6. Application Procedures
(1) Applicants should complete the application form via the WEB Application System. All the required
documents must be uploaded as PDF files to the WEB Application System. Follow the instructions on the
admission webpage of the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology (admission webpage
https://www.i.u-tokyo.ac.jp/edu/entra/entra_e.shtml) and on the WEB application system. Access the
system from the admission webpage.
(2) Application Period
1) Summer Entrance Examinations
Applications are accepted from Friday, May 29, until 14:00 on Thursday, June 4, 2026 (JST).
2) Winter Entrance Examinations
Applications are accepted from Wednesday, November 11, until 14:00 on Tuesday, November 17, 2026
(JST).

Department Overview
The mission of Department of Computer Science is to contribute to research and education on
fundamental theories of computer science, their implementations as computer systems, and
applications to natural science, which provide foundations for information technologies
indispensable in today’s society. Of particular interest is in-depth research on the core of
information and computation rather than the use of information technologies as a tool. The
research focus includes, but is not limited to: Discrete Mathematics, Numerical Algorithms,
Programming Languages, Algorithms, Quantum Computation, Computer Architecture,
Operating Systems, Software, Machine Learning, Artificial Intelligence, Bioinformatics,
Computational Science, Natural Language Processing, Information Retrieval, Computer
Vision, Human Computer Interaction, and Graphics. For more details, refer to the document
“Faculty and Labs in Department of Computer Science” provided separately.
Master’s Program
(1) Introduction
A. Department of Computer Science conducts Summer Entrance Examinations. The
department does not receive applications for Master’s program in Winter Entrance
Examination.
B. Before sending Application Form, the applicants (except those who are completing the
Undergraduate Program of Department of Information Science in the Faculty of
Science at the University of Tokyo) should contact at least one of their prospective
supervisors.
C. Check the document “Faculty and Labs in Department of Computer Science” and
enter your selection of (up to six) labs through the Web application system.
Prepare a Research Plan document following the guideline on page 9 and submit it
through the Web application system.
(2) Examination Schedule
(2.1) Summer Entrance Examinations

Document screening will be conducted based on the submitted documents. Regarding
the notification of the screening, refer to the Admission Guide of the graduate school.
Applicants who have failed the document screening are not eligible for the written
and oral examinations.

Doctoral Program and Doctoral Program (Special
Selection for Professionals)
(1) Introduction
A. Department of Computer Science conducts Summer Entrance Examinations and
Winter Entrance Examinations for its Doctoral Program. The schedules and
examination methods differ between Summer and Winter Entrance Examinations.
B. Before sending an Application Form, applicants must contact their prospective
supervisor and show their research proposals.
C. Check the document "Faculty and Labs in Department of Computer Science" and
enter the name of your prospective supervisor in the field “Supervising Professor of
your choice” of the Web application system. Prepare a Research Plan document based
on the guidelines given on page 9, and submit it through the Web application system.
D. For Summer Entrance Examinations:
I. Those who have completed or are expected to complete the Master’s program in
Department of Computer Science in the Graduate School of Information Science
and Technology at the University of Tokyo, or Department of Information Science
in the Graduate School of Science at the University of Tokyo, are exempted from
the following:
✓ Submission of the TOEFL score
✓ Written Examinations.
E. For Winter Entrance Examinations:
I. Together with the application form, submit a Letter of Recommendation. The
letter should be submitted directly by your recommender. Typically, but not
necessarily, the recommender is an academic who knows you well, such as your
current supervisor. The recommender should not be a faculty member of
Department of Computer Science, the University of Tokyo.
II. Those who have completed or are expected to complete the Master’s program in
Department of Computer Science in the Graduate School of Information Science
and Technology at the University of Tokyo, or Department of Information Science
in the Graduate School of Science at the University of Tokyo, are exempted from
the following:
✓ Submission of the TOEFL score
✓ Submission of the Letter of Recommendation
✓ Oral Examination 1.
(2) Examination Schedule
(2.1) Summer Entrance Examinations
① Document Screening

eligibility for the Written and Oral Examinations will be announced on the website

from 15:00, Tuesday, July 21st, 2026 (JST), for the period of three days (72 hours).
The announcement will be made by using the examinee number that is found on the
examination admission card delivered in advance.

③ Oral Examination 2
The examination is conducted by oral presentation of a Master’s thesis or its
alternative as described below, in addition to questions on the topics that are relevant
to applicants' research fields. This examination will be conducted at the same time as
the oral examination 1 above. For those who are completing the Master’s Program of
Department of Computer Science in the Graduate School of Information Science and
Technology at the University of Tokyo, examinations may be conducted in a different
schedule and style; in that case, they will be separately notified.
I. Present the research from your Master’s program in 12 minutes and the research
plan for your doctoral program in 3 minutes. Research results after completion
of Master’s program can be included. Each examinee should prepare a PC for the
presentation.
II. Submit PDF copies of Master’s thesis (which can be in any other language than
Japanese and English) and of a few published papers on the presented research,
if any, to the department in advance. The guideline of PDF submission will be
-7-
notified later. Those who have not yet written a Master’s thesis at the time of the
oral examination should submit a plan of the Master’s thesis (which should
summarize the research goal and method, the results obtained so far, etc. in
English in a free format; the main text, excluding the title page and references,
should be at least 6 pages in A4 size) as an alternative. Those who have not
written a Master’s thesis and do not plan to write it (such as those whose
eligibility to apply for the Doctoral program is not based on a Master’s degree)
should contact the department admission office in advance on alternative
documents to be submitted.

Guidelines for Preparing the Research Plan Document
For Master’s Program
Describe your research plan in the following format in A4 size and submit it with the
Application Form. The description should be two pages long (excluding references) in
Japanese or English. Plagiarism is strictly prohibited.
Research Plan
Name: Xxx Yyy (your name)
New information processing is enabled by the progress of computers. Still there remains a
problem of …. In Master’s Course I want to tackle this problem based on the following ideas:
(For example, describe the research plan with required specialized knowledge, related work,
and/or programming skills)
(1) …
References
[1] …
(Describe your notable achievements if any)
Notable achievements: …
For Doctoral Program and Doctoral Program [Special Selection for Professionals]
Describe your research results, your major publications, and your research plan for the
Doctoral Program (try lucid explanations, e.g., using figures) in the following format in A4
size. The description should be four pages long (excluding references) in Japanese or English.
Submit it with the Application Form. Plagiarism is strictly prohibited.
Research Plan
Name: Xxx Yyy (your name)
1. Research results
1.1 Fast algorithm of linear solution [1]
We have developed a fast algorithm for solution of large scale systems of linear equations…
1.2 Summary of study or research achievements during your period of employment
(Include this section if you are applying for the Special Selection for Professionals)
2. Selected Publication list
[1] Xxx Yyy, "Fast algorithm of linear solution," Master’s thesis, Graduate School of Information
Science and Technology, the University of Tokyo (2011).
[2] Xxx Yyy and Aaa Bbb, "Fast algorithm of linear solution," proceedings of 2011 annual meeting of
research society, pp. 101-102, 2011.
3. Research Plan
In Doctoral Program I am planning to improve the above algorithm to solve larger problem…
References
[1] …

Admissions for the Year 2027 (2026 August and 2027 February)
Applicants must also see the admission guide of Graduate School of Information
Science and Technology.
Admission Guide
Please make sure to obtain the admission guides for both the school and the department.

Admission guide for the school
Important Dates
Entrance Examination Briefing (Graduate School)
Please refer to Admission guide for the school
Entrance Examination Briefing (Department)
Date
2:30pm - 3:30pm, May 23, 2026
Place
6F Hiloby, IREF Building, Hongo Campus (No. 98 in the map)
Online: https://u-tokyo-ac-jp.zoom.us/j/86808495174?pwd=GxWgippefFBJoOrAnGjBBFtHtWUuia.1
Summer Entrance Exam
Application
May 29, 2026 - 14:00 (JST), June 4, 2026
Winter Entrance Exam
Application
November 11, 2026 - 14:00 (JST), November 17, 2026
For details, please refer to the admission guides for both the school and the department. In the case of conflict between those admission guides and this page, the admission guides shall prevail and be conclusive.
The past entrance exams (including English versions) are available at the bottom of this page.

When the server for a site differs from the www address given above, or even when it is i.u-tokyo.ac.jp, individual sites are subject to separate privacy policies. See each site for further information. Individual pages may include terms that override this privacy policy. Priority is given to the terms posted on individual pages.

AY2027 Admission Guide
Department of Mathematical Informatics
Graduate School of Information Science and Technology
The University of Tokyo
Master’s Program
Doctoral Program
Contact
Department Team (Department of Mathematical Informatics),
Academic Affairs Group, Graduate School of Engineering / Information Science and Technology,
The University of Tokyo,
7-3-1 Hongo, Bunkyo-ku, Tokyo 113-8656, JAPAN.
E-mail: office@office.keisu.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp
Visit
https://www.i.u-tokyo.ac.jp/edu/entra/index_e.shtml
to refer to the admission guide of the Graduate School of Information
Science and Technology and the admission guide of each department.
Note: In addition to this brochure, be sure to refer to the admission guide of the Graduate School of
Information Science and Technology, the University of Tokyo. Regarding inquiries about the entrance
examinations, e-mail inquiries will be accepted in principle. Replies will be made from 9 a.m. to 5 p.m.
on weekdays in Japan Standard Time. Make sure that it may take several days to receive the replies.
This document is an unofficial translation from the official Japanese version.
(I) Master’s Program [Summer Entrance Examinations]
1. Outline
Selection will be conducted based on the “AY 2027 Admission Guide: Master’s Program, Graduate
School of Information Science and Technology, The University of Tokyo” (posted on https://www.i.u-
tokyo.ac.jp/edu/entra/entra_e.shtml), and on this admission guide of the Department of Mathematical
Informatics. All important items common to the graduate school, including requirements for eligibility
and application procedures, are listed in the above common guide, so be sure to refer to that document.
This admission guide supplements the above common guide with explanations regarding examination
procedures that are specific to the Department of Mathematical Informatics.
Based on the interdisciplinary nature of the Department of Mathematical Informatics, this department
invites applicants from a wide range of fields, both inside and outside the University of Tokyo, with a
fundamental understanding and knowledge of mathematics, informatics, and physics. Examination
subjects have been selected to ensure that applicants with diverse backgrounds can take examinations
under fair conditions.
2. Declaring preferred faculty advisors, etc.
Declare the following items in the designated fields in the Web application system.
・Preferred faculty advisors: Choose your first to sixth preferences from the pull-down menu in the
field “Your Selection of Lab” on the application system. For the available faculty advisors and their
research areas, consult the list in this Admission Guide. If you have any preferred choices in addition
to the first six choices, fill (list) the information in the following Field 1 for Department-Specific
Information. The choice of preferred faculty advisors does not affect the result.
・ Field 1 for Department-Specific Information: As mentioned above, fill (list) the additional
information on the preferred faculty advisors, if there are any in addition to the first six preferred
choices.
・Field 2 for Department-Specific Information: Declare which language you hope to use in the oral
examination, Japanese or English.
3. Examination Subjects
3.1．Document screening
Document screening will be conducted based on the submitted documents. Regarding the notification
of the screening results, refer to the admission guide of the Graduate School of Information Science
and Technology.
Applicants are required to submit essays at the time of application, following “Document Screening
Assignment (for Master’s Program)” on page 10 of this guide.
3.2．General education subjects
We will conduct a mathematics examination. The examination will be conducted only for applicants
who have passed the document screening. Refer to the admission guide of the Graduate School of
Information Science and Technology for details.
3.3. Foreign language (English)
TOEFL scores will be used to evaluate applicants’ English skills. For details, refer to “Guidelines for
Submission of TOEFL Scores (for AY2027 Entrance Examinations)” which is provided on the
1
This document is an unofficial translation from the official Japanese version.
webpage of the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology.
3.4. Specialized subjects and oral examination
The examinations will be conducted only for applicants who have passed the document screening and
taken the written examinations of the general education subjects.
1) Written Examination
For examinations on the specialized subjects, applicants shall choose one subject from among the
following: “Mathematical Informatics” and “Information Physics and Computing.” Please note that
dates, times, and locations differ depending on the subjects.
Specialized subjects Examination Dates, Scope of questions
Times, and Locations
Mathematical August 24, 2026 Applicants will be required to solve three out of five
Informatics (Monday) problems related to mathematical methods for
10:00 – 13:00 solving problems, including: algebraic methods,
Faculty of analytic methods, geometric methods, discrete
Engineering Bldg. methods, stochastic methods, statistical methods,
No. 6 and algorithms, etc.
Information Physics Please refer to the “Admission Guide” of Department of Information Physics
and Computing and Computing.
・Applicants will be notified of details for the examination location for “Mathematical Informatics”
by August 12 (Wednesday), 2026.
・For information on the examination locations for “Information Physics and Computing,” refer to
the “Admission Guide” of Department of Information Physics and Computing.
2) Oral examination
The oral examination will be held online on August 27 (Thursday), 2026 in principle. Examinees will
be notified of the instructions of the oral examination by early August.
The oral examination will be conducted only for applicants who have taken the written examinations
for both general education subjects and specialized subjects. Examinees will be notified regarding the
detailed schedule of the oral examination by noon of August 12 (Wednesday), 2026. The schedule
cannot be adjusted. Please take the examination on the date and time specified.
4. Important Notes
4.1. Those who are absent from one of the exams (the general education subjects, the written
examination on the specialized subjects, and the oral examination) will be considered to have
withdrawn from the entrance examination.
4.2. Those who have not submitted their TOEFL scores by the deadline specified by the graduate
school will not be permitted to take the written exams (the general education subjects and the
specialized subjects).
4.3. During the written examinations, applicants can use only writing instruments (pencils or
mechanical pencils; all must be black), pencil sharpeners, erasers, and a clock or watch (only with
timekeeping function). No other instruments can be used.
4.4. For other notes, be sure to carefully read the “Department of Mathematical Informatics
2
This document is an unofficial translation from the official Japanese version.
Information for applicants taking the Entrance Examinations Onsite,” which is included on page 8 of
this Guide.
4.5. Winter entrance examinations are not conducted for the master's program.
5. Contact
If you have any questions regarding this admission guide, contact the Department Team indicated on
the cover.
(II) Doctoral Program [Summer and Winter Entrance Examinations]
1. Outline
Selection will be conducted based on the “AY 2027 Admission Guide: Doctoral Program, Graduate
School of Information Science and Technology, The University of Tokyo” (posted on https://www.i.u-
tokyo.ac.jp/edu/entra/index_e.shtml), and on this admission guide of the Department of Mathematical
Informatics. All important items common to the graduate school, including requirements for eligibility
and application procedures, are listed in the above common guide, so be sure to refer to that document.
This admission guide supplements the above common guide with explanations regarding examination
procedures that are specific to the Department of Mathematical Informatics.
2. Contact before the Submission of the Applications
Applicants are required to meet with their preferred faculty advisor prior to the submission of their
applications.
3. Declaring a preferred faculty advisor, etc.
Declare the following items in the designated fields in the Web application system.
・Preferred faculty advisor: Indicate one in the field “Supervising Professor of your choice” on the
application system.
・Field 1 for the Department-Specific Information: Declare which language you hope to use in the
oral examination, Japanese or English.
4. Documents
Applicants are required to write reports on the following themes and submit them with the application
documents. The reports should be organized so that their answers to each of the questions ((1), (2),
(3)(a)–(e)) are shown to be clear.
Theme:
(1) Summarize your past research achievements and research plans for the Doctoral Program in a
report in Japanese or English, comprising no more than ten A4-sized pages, including formulas,
diagrams, etc. Include a list of your past research achievements at the end of the report. The list
includes academic papers, research presentations, patents, products, and other examples of the
applicant's achievements. In the case of collaborative achievements involving several persons, clearly
indicate the part and the weight of the applicant's contributions for each achievement. Applicants may
use any format appropriate for this report.
(2) Provide an English summary of the above report of about one A4-sized page.
3
This document is an unofficial translation from the official Japanese version.
(3) Provide responses to the following questions regarding the content of the above report in Japanese
or English, comprising no more than two A4-sized pages.
(a) What is the goal of your research?
(b) What are the specific contents of this research and the methods used?
(c) What is the current status of this field of research, in Japan and overseas?
(d) What aspects of this research could be considered new in comparison to past researches in this
field?
(e) How will the results of this research contribute to the field in question?
5. Selection Methods
5.1. Foreign language (English)
・TOEFL scores will be used to evaluate applicants’ English skills. For details, refer to “Guidelines
for Submission of TOEFL Scores (for AY2027 Entrance Examinations)” which is provided on the
webpage of the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology.
・For applicants who have completed or are expected to complete a Master's Program in the University
of Tokyo, the submission is waived.
5.2. Written examination
・ Applicants will take an examination on the specialized subject “Mathematical Informatics.”
Applicants will be required to answer essay-type questions that assess specialized academic skills and
knowledge.
・In the Summer Entrance Examination, the written examination will be held online on August 25
(Tuesday), 2026, in principle. Applicants will be notified of the detailed schedule and the method of
the examination by early August.
・In the Winter Entrance Examination, the written examination will be held online between January
26 (Tuesday) and January 29 (Friday), 2027, in principle. Applicants will be notified of the detailed
schedule and the method of the examination by the middle of January.
・Applicants who have completed or are expected to complete a Master's Program of the University
of Tokyo may be exempted from the specialized subject written examination. The decision regarding
the exemption will be notified to the applicants around mid-July for the summer entrance examination
and around late December for the winter entrance examination.
5.3. Oral examination
・Applicants will be required to answer questions on the research plan and other aspects of the
submitted documents. Applicants should prepare presentation slides summarizing the descriptions on
your past research achievements and future research plans for the Doctoral Program in the submitted
documents.
・In the Summer Entrance Examination, the oral examination will be held online between August 25
(Tuesday) and August 26 (Wednesday), 2026, in principle. Applicants will be notified of the detailed
schedule and the method of the examination by early August.
・In the Winter Entrance Examination, the oral examination will be held online between January 27
(Wednesday) and January 29 (Friday), 2027, in principle. Applicants will be notified regarding the
detailed schedule and the method of the examination by the middle of January.
4
This document is an unofficial translation from the official Japanese version.
6. Contact
If you have any questions regarding this admission guide, please contact the Department Team as
indicated on the cover.
(Ⅲ) Doctoral Program - Special Selection for Professionals [Summer
and Winter Entrance Examinations]
1. Outline
Selection will be conducted based on the “AY 2027 Admission Guide: Doctoral Program [Special
Selection for Professionals], Graduate School of Information Science and Technology, The University
of Tokyo” (posted on https://www.i.u-tokyo.ac.jp/edu/entra/index_e.shtml) and on this admission
guide of the Department of Mathematical Informatics. All important items common to the graduate
school, including requirements for eligibility and application procedures, are listed in the above
common guides, so please be sure to refer to those documents. This admission guide supplements the
above common guides with explanations regarding examination procedures that are specific to the
Department of Mathematical Informatics.
2. Contact before the Submission of the Applications
Applicants are required to meet with their preferred faculty advisor prior to the submission of their
applications.
3. Declaring a preferred faculty advisor, etc.
Declare the following items in the designated fields in the WEB Application System.
・Preferred faculty advisor: Indicate one in the field “Supervising Professor of your choice” on the
application system.
・Field 1 for the Department-Specific Information: Declare which language you hope to use in the
oral examination, Japanese or English.
4. Documents
Applicants are required to prepare for the following five items regarding applicants' research and work
achievements in a field related to Mathematical Informatics.
(1) List of achievements
Provide a list of academic papers, research presentations, patents, products, and other examples of the
applicant's achievements. In the case of collaborative achievements involving several persons, clearly
indicate the part and the weight of the applicant's contributions.
(2) Outline of main achievements
Select three or fewer main achievements from the above list, and provide an outline of

On the Entrance Examination Briefing for AY2027 Entrance ExaminationsÂ (released on March 9, 2026)
Entrance Examination Briefing for AY2027 Entrance Examinations will be held on Saturday, May 23, 2026.
Those who wish to participate in the briefing should submit the entry form.
Please refer toÂ Entrance Examination BriefingÂ for further information.
[Important] On the expected changes from AY2027 Entrance Examinations (conducted in AY2026)Â (released on June 13, 2025)
We expect the following changes in the AY2027 entrance examinations (conducted in AY2026) of our graduate school. For details, please refer to the admission guide of the graduate school and the admission guide of each department, which will be released in late April 2026 (TBD).
Winter entrance examination for the Master's program of Department of Information and Communication Engineering shall not be conducted. Applications for the Master's program shall be accepted only for the summer entrance examination.
For the summer entrance examination of the Masterâs program of the Department of Mathematical Informatics, applicants shall select one of the following two specialized subjects at the time of application: âMathematical Informaticsâ and âInformation Physics and Computingâ.
For the summer entrance examination of the Masterâs program of the Department of Creative Informatics, applicants shall select one of the following four specialized subjects at the time of application: âCreative Informaticsâ, âComputer Scienceâ, âMathematical Informaticsâ, and âInformation Physics and Computingâ.
The same changes are expected to apply to AY2028 (conducted in AY2027) and later entrance examinations as well.
[Important] Delivering Lectures in EnglishÂ (released on March 25, 2025)
From April 2026, most lectures at the graduate school will be delivered in English. This transformation is on purpose not only to improve learning environment for international students drastically, but also to provide Japanese students with opportunity to train their English communication skills, which are indispensable to research activity in information science and technology.
Overview of the Entrance Examination System
An overview of the types of entrance examinations and the schedule for AY2027 entrance examinations (conducted in AY2026) at this Graduate School isÂ given in the following document.
Overview of the Entrance Examination System at the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology, The University of Tokyo
Japanese Government (Ministry of Education, Culture, Sports, Science and Technology, MEXT) Scholarship students (Research Students) (Embassy Recommendation) will be accepted into this graduate school as âGraduate International Research Students (non-degree)â, in principle.Â Please refer toÂ hereÂ for more details. If the applicant wishes to proceed to the Master's or Doctoral programs after they entered as a Graduate International Research Student, they will need to take and pass the entrance examinations (Regular Admission or Special Selection for Professionals).
* AY stands for "academic year". In Japan, each academic year is from April to March in the next year.

Application Procedures
The Examination Admission Card and other important notices will be delivered to each applicant via E-mail to the address stated on the application form. Be sure to configure your E-mail settings so that you can receive E-mails from the domain u-tokyo.ac.jp and receive notifications when they arrive.
Please ensure our emails are not stored in the spam folder.
Those who wish to apply for the AY2027 Winter Entrance Examinations (conducted in AY2026) should follow the procedures below.
1. Prepare the necessary materials for your application
Check the Admission Guide of the Graduate School and your preferred department and prepare the necessary materials for your application.
Please keep the original of the Academic Transcript submitted as a copy during the admission process, as it is required at the time of enrollment procedure.Â The original of Certificate of Graduation/Completion/Degree isÂ also required at the time of enrollment procedure.
Checklist for Application Documents (Masterâs Program)
Checklist for Application Documents (Doctoral Program)
Checklist for Application Documents (Doctoral Program [Special Selection for Professionals])
2. Enter the required information to the WEB Application System below and create the application form
ãavailable period : Wednesday, November 11 - 14:00 on Tuesday, November 17, 2026 (JST)ã
WEB Application System (Masterâs Program)
WEB Application System (Doctoral Program)
WEB Application System (Doctoral Program [Special Selection for Professionals])
3. Pay your examination fee
1) Bank Transfer (in Japan):
Use the Transfer Application Form from the "WEB Application System" and ask a teller to transfer your examination fee.
2) E-shiharai.net (by credit card / at a convenience store, etc.):
Refer to "How to make Payment of Examination Fee at E-shiharai.net" and pay your examination fee as instructed.

Graduate School of Engineering｜Degree Students
International Admission Programs (IAP)
TOP > Prospective Students > Graduate Programs > Admissions > Degree Students > International Admissions > International Admission Programs
ADMISSIONS FOR DEGREE STUDENTS
Regular Admission
International Admission

The Graduate School of Engineering offers International Admission Program (IAP) as a pathway for degree-seeking international students residing overseas.
This pathway is offered in 14 departments within the School of Engineering, as detailed in "2. Application Guidelines for Individual Programs" below.
Why Choose IAP?
There is no need to travel to Japan before entrance (Online Application and Selection)
No knowledge of Japanese Language is required
Once admitted, students will pursue their Master’s or Doctoral degrees alongside those enrolled through the regular admission process. The vast majority of courses at the Graduate School of Engineering are conducted in English, ensuring accessibility for non-Japanese speakers.
1. General Application Guidelines
2. Application Guidelines for Individual IAPs
3. Online Application System: T-cens
4. Payment of Examination Fee
1. General Application Guidelines
All applicants, regardless of the department they are applying to, are required to read either of the following.
General Application Guidelines for Master’s Programs
General Application Guidelines for Doctoral Programs
＊Applicants to the IAP are required to satisfy both of the requirements set forth in 1. General Application Guidelines and 2. Application Guidelines for Individual IAPs. Applicants are requested to carefully review both guidelines prior to applying.
2. Application Guidelines for Individual IAPs
Online application to each department should be made through the respective IAPs shown in the table below. Once applicants have decided on the program through which they wish to apply, they must carefully read the application guidelines of individual IAPs where they can confirm details including application deadlines, eligibility, required documents, financial aid and admission procedure.
Master's Degree Programs

Graduate Program for International Students in Urban and Environmental Studies

International Graduate Program in Chemistry and Biotechnology
gakumu[at]chem.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp
-
＊When you send an email, please replace [at] with @
3. Online Application System: T-cens
Eligible international applicants should apply for IAPs through our online system “T-cens” to receive a preliminary evaluation based on academic qualification.
*T-cens is not applicable to the International Graduate Program in Chemistry and Biothechnology.
Please read the T-cens Guidance Information carefully before completing all the required fields and documents.
4. Payment of Examination Fee
How to Make a Payment of Examination Fee at E-shiharai net.pdf

=== https://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/hubfs/admission/2026/international-admission/II%E3%80%90%E5%85%B1%E9%80%9A%E3%80%91General%20Application%20Guidelines%202027_Doctoral%20Degree.pdf (status=200, final=https://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/hubfs/admission/2026/international-admission/II%E3%80%90%E5%85%B1%E9%80%9A%E3%80%91General%20Application%20Guidelines%202027_Doctoral%20Degree.pdf, len=23673) ===
General Application Guidelines
for International Admission Programs
Doctoral Degree
Graduate School of Engineering, The University of Tokyo
April / October 2027 Entry
Education and Research Goals
The education and research goals at this graduate school are to develop talented individuals who,
with a basis in engineering, boldly take up the challenge of exploring unexplored fields and new
technological innovations, contribute to the sustainability and development of human society, and who
are able to acquire systematic knowledge regarding science and technology as well as an engineering-
based thinking, and have the ability to solve social problems with an understanding of diversity and a
broad perspective.
Admissions Policy
1. Desired student profile
(1) Strongly motivated to study in accordance with the educational research goals of the Graduate
School.
(2) In addition to advanced expertise in one’s major field of study, possesses engineering and logical
thinking skills as well as creativity, and has a strong desire to solve social problems.
(3) Possesses excellent communication skills, foreign language proficiencies, and sound ethics,
respects diversity, and aspires to contribute to the international community.
2. Selection Policy
Applicants are selected through written and oral examinations, and submitted documents are
evaluated based on the desired student profile. Academic records and foreign language skills are taken
into consideration as well. In this selection process, emphasis will be placed on the following.
・High level of expertise in field of interest, and deep engineering-based thinking skills.
・Ability to discover unknown problems and develop creative solutions.
・International communication skills and ability to disseminate research results through those skills.
1. Introduction: International Admission Programs and Courses
This document outlines general requirements applicable to International Admission Programs of the
Graduate School of Engineering, the University of Tokyo. Before applying, prospective applicants should
also read the application guidelines of individual programs listed below, which include details on the
application period, documents for submission, selection period, and result announcement. Application
guidelines of individual programs can be found on the following website.
https://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/study-at-utokyo/soe/international-programs

However, applicants applying for one of the courses listed below are allowed to apply for CSC

2. Eligibility
Applicants must satisfy all the eligibility requirements for the Doctoral Degree International Admission
Programs which are mentioned below in articles (1) and (2). At the same time, applicants must fulfill at
least one of the eligibility requirements described in articles (3) to (6). Those who have graduated from
undergraduate universities in Japan (including the University of Tokyo), or who are enrolled in universities
in Japan (including non-regular students such as research students) are not eligible for application
through International Admission Programs (Note 1).
(1) A person who neither has Japanese nationality nor resides in Japan during the period of time from
the online application’s start date until its deadline, i.e., during the application period on T-cens
(※1,2,3).
※ 1 Multiple citizenship holders with Japanese nationality and nationality from another
country (or countries) will, if unable to provide proof of relinquishment of Japanese
nationality by the time of arrival in Japan, be regarded as ineligible for the program.
※ 2 A person who resides in Japan (meaning a person who has a status of residence provided
for by the “Immigration Control and Refugee Recognition Act (Cabinet Order No. 319 of
1951)” of Japan) during the application period on T-cens is not eligible. However, the
following students may be allowed to apply as an exception.
・Non-Japanese international students who are temporarily enrolled at the University of
Tokyo during the application period on T-cens through an exchange or short-term
program with a partner institution, and who can provide proof of their primary affiliation
outside Japan.
・Should these students wish to apply, they must submit documents showing that they are
participating in a program administered by the University of Tokyo.
※3 Those who neither have Japanese nationality nor reside in Japan during the application
period on T-cens but have “permanent resident”, “long-term resident”, or “special
permanent resident” residence status in Japan are in principle ineligible to apply.
However, individuals who correspond to Eligibilities (4) through (6) below may be
allowed to apply. For details, please contact the office of each program no later than 2
weeks prior to the deadline for online application via T-cens. Those who have
graduated from universities with a bachelor’s degree, a master’s degree or a doctoral
degree in Japan (including the University of Tokyo) are not eligible.
(2) A person with the language proficiency to take classes and receive research guidance in English.
(3) A person who has been conferred, or is expected to be conferred by the day before the admission
date in April or October 2027, a degree equivalent to a master’s, doctoral or a professional degree in
a country other than Japan. A person who has graduated or who is expecting to graduate from
universities in Japan (including the University of Tokyo) with a bachelor’s degree, master’s degree or
doctoral degree is not eligible. (Notes 2, 3).
(4) A person who has passed or is expected to pass by the day before the admission date in April or
October 2027, a screening for fundamental ability for doctorate dissertation research as specified in
Article 16-2 of the Standards for Establishment of Graduate Schools at a school abroad, shall be
recognized by this graduate school as having abilities at least equivalent to those who have a
master’s degree. (Notes 3, 5).
(5) A person who has completed at least 16 years of academic education in a country other than Japan,
and has been engaged in research at a university, research institute or other institution in a country
other than Japan for at least 2 years, or is expected to have been engaged in research for at least 2
years by the day before the admission date in April or October 2027, and shall be recognized by this
graduate school as having abilities at least equivalent to those who have a master’s degree or a
professional degree as a result of the said research (Notes 3, 5).
3
(6) A person who is 24 years of age or older as of the day before the admission date in April or October
2027, whom this graduate school has recognized, through individual screening of entrance
qualifications, as having abilities that are at least equivalent to a master’s degree or a professional
degree holder (Notes 3, 4, 5, 6).
Note 1 Japanese universities mentioned above are universities within the boundaries of Japan and
are as prescribed in Article 83 of the School Education Act.
Note 2 Those who have obtained a master’s, a professional degree, or its equivalent from other
Japanese universities and who reside outside Japan during the application period on T-cens
are eligible to apply to this program at the discretion of the department they are applying to.
Even in such cases, those who have graduated from undergraduate universities in Japan
(including the University of Tokyo) are not eligible to apply.
Note 3 Although the admission date is scheduled to be on April 1 or October 1 2027, it may be
subject to change. The confirmed admission date will be announced on our web site later.
Note 4 Those covered under Eligibility (6) above are those who are not covered under Eligibility (3)
through (5) above, but are recognized by this graduate school through individual screening
as having abilities at least equivalent to that of a master’s degree or a professional degree
holder.
Note 5 Those who wish to apply based on Eligibility (4), (5) or (6) must contact the office of each
program no later than 2 weeks prior to the deadline for online application via T-cens, as the
eligibility for application is confirmed individually.
Note 6 Those who are recognized as having abilities that are at least equivalent to a master’s degree
or a professional degree holder through screening of entrance qualifications will be permitted
to apply and take the entrance examination.

This document is a translation from the authoritative Japanese version.
Graduate School of Engineering, The University of Tokyo
Guidelines for Applicants to AY 2027 Doctoral Program
・If the content of the Guidelines for Applicants is subject to change, the change will be announced on the
following webpage. Applicants should periodically check the page.
(https://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/study-at-utokyo/soe/apply/guideline)
・Following the submission of application, applicants will receive a notification regarding their application
and the entrance examination via e-mail. Applicants must ensure their e-mail settings allow them to receive
messages from both the WEB Application System(application.t[at]ic.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp) and The Graduate
School of Engineering Team (daigakuin.t[at].gs.mail.u-tokyo.ac.jp). ※Please replace [at] with @.
・Even for departments whose enrollment capacity is not listed in “6. Examination Subjects and Enrollment
Capacity for each Department” for schedule B, admission maybe offered under Application Schedule B
(Winter exam). If so, it will be announced on the above webpage around October 1.
Notes for applicants who have foreign nationality
It usually takes about two to three months to obtain a new status of residence following the issuance of the
Notification of Admission. Those who need to apply for a Certificate of Eligibility(COE) through this
university for enrollment are advised to consider opting for April admission.
Education and Research Goals
The education and research goals at this graduate school are to develop talented individuals who, with a
basis in engineering, boldly take up the challenge of exploring unexplored fields and new technological
innovations, contribute to the sustainability and development of human society, and who are able to acquire
systematic knowledge regarding science and technology as well as an engineering -based thinking, and have
the ability to solve social problems with an understanding of diversity and a broad perspe ctive.
Admission Policy
1. Desired student profile
1) Strongly motivated to study in accordance with the educational research goals of the Graduate School.
2) In addition to advanced expertise in one’s major field of study, possesses engineering and logical
thinking skills as well as creativity, and has a strong desire to solve social problems.
3) Possesses excellent communication skills, foreign language proficiencies, and sound ethics, respects
diversity, and aspires to contribute to the international community.
2. Selection Policy
Applicants are selected through written and oral examinations, and submitted documents are evaluated
based on the desired student profile. Academic records and foreign language skills are taken into
consideration as well. In this selection process, empha sis will be placed on the following.
・High level of expertise in field of interest, and deep engineering -based thinking skills.
・Ability to discover unknown problems and develop creative solutions.
・International communication skills and ability to disseminate research results through those skills.
1. Requirements for Eligibility
- 1 -
This document is a translation from the authoritative Japanese version.
(1) Those who have obtained, or are expected to obtain by March 31, 2027, a master’s or professional degree
from a Japanese university. (Note 1) (Note 5)
(2) Those who have been conferred or are expected to be conferred on or before March 31,2027, a master’s
degree by the National Institution for Academic Degrees and Quality Enhancement of Higher Education
(NIAD). (Note 5)
(3) Those who have been conferred or are expected to be conferred on or before March 31, 2027, a degree

professional degree. (Note 5)

2. Acceptance of Mature Students
(1) Mature students are eligible for acceptance in each department. Departments shall accept mature students.
(2) Persons currently working as full-time employees at companies, educational and research institutes, and
other organizations may undertake studies in this doctoral program, concurrent with such employment.
(3) Selection of applicants shall take into consideration factors including the applicant's master's thesis and
related research achievements.
3. How to Apply
The periods for acceptance of applications are divided into two categories - Application Schedule A and
Application Schedule B - as indicated below (2).
However, Application Schedule B does not apply to some departments, so please confirm with the
department intended for application in advance. Applicants will not be prevented from applying during both
acceptance periods, but persons wishing to enter the School in October 2026 shall be subject to the
limitations of Application Schedule A.
(1) Applicants must create an entrance application form via the WEB Application System. All required
documents must be converted into PDF format and uploaded to the system. For further details, please
follow the instructions provided on the Graduate School’s website and the Web A pplication System. The
system can be accessed through the Graduate School’s website : https://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/study-at-
utokyo/soe/apply/guideline
(2) Application period
Application Schedule A: From May 29 (Friday) until 3:00 p.m., June 4 (Thursday), 2026 (JST)【Strict
Deadline】
Application Schedule B: From November 27 (Friday) until 3:00 p.m., December 3 (Thursday), 2026 (JST)
【Strict Deadline】

4. Examination Schedule and Venue
(1) Application Schedule A:
The Primary examination will be held over five days, from August 31 (Monday) through September 4
(Friday), 2026. For the examination schedule and venue, refer to the “Guide to Entrance Examinations” of
the department to which the application will be sent.
As a general rule, the written examinations of General education subjects (General academic) will be held
at specified venues (on the Hongo campus).
The Secondary examinations will be conducted between mid-January and early February, 2027. Details
regarding examination dates, timetable and venue will be notified to eligible applicants by around early
January 2027. However, for applicants falling under either of the following categories, the secondary
examination will be held over five days, from August 31 (Monday) to September 4 (Friday), 2026.
① Those who have already obtained a master’s degree or professional degree at the time of application,
or who are expected to receive a master’s degree or professional degree by September 30, 2026. (Note
1)
② Those who wish to enroll in October 2026.
Note 1) Secondary examinations will, in some cases, take place sometime between mid-January and early February, even if
candidates fall under ①. Please refer to the “Guide to Entrance Examinations” from the department to which application will
be sent, or to the notice provided along with the examination admission card.
(2) Application Schedule B:
Primary and secondary examinations will be held between mid-January 2027 and early February 2027.
Please refer to the "Guide to Entrance Examinations" of the department to which the application will be
sent. Candidates will be advised of examination dates and other relevant information in December 2026,
when examination admission card notices will be provided.
5. Selection Methods
(1) Selection shall be based on primary and secondary examinations.
(2) Selection for the primary examination shall be based on a written examination, an oral examination, and
submitted documents.
(3) Some departments make selections based on submitted documents. Written examinations, and oral
examinations may be conducted only for those who have passed the selections based on their documents.
Examination fees will not be refunded even if applicants do not pass the document -based selections. For
details, refer to the “Guide to Entrance Examinations” of the department to which the application will be
sent.
(4) The secondary examination shall be in regard to the applicant ’s master's thesis or a related topic. The
secondary examination will only be conducted for those who pass the primary examination.

(5) Selection of students for admission shall be based on a comprehensive judgment, taking into account the
written examination, the oral examination, submitted documents, the applicant's master's thesis and other
related materials.

INTERNATIONAL GRADUATE PROGRAM
The Department of Civil Engineering at the University of Tokyo boasts one of the most advanced and progressive global academic environments in the world, and has admission systems to recruit talented students internationally. In order to reach the global talent pool, the Department of Civil Engineering recruits students through both international admission and regular admission. International students living outside Japan can apply for international admission where applicants do not need to travel to Tokyo to take entrance exams. These international students can alternatively choose to take entrance exams for regular admission. On the other hand, international students already in Japan can apply only for regular admission and must take the university’s entrance exams.
Prospective students who wish to obtain international admission and study at the Department of Civil Engineering must obtain a full-scholarship as shown in the figure below, which also provides details of the three categories of scholarship available:
Download Brochure
ELIGIBILITY
Applicants must satisfy all of the eligibility requirements for the International Graduate Program of the Department of Civil Engineering as mentioned below in Articles (1.) to (3.). In addition, applicants must also fulfil one of the eligibility requirements described in Articles (4.) and (5.).
1.A foreign national who is not a permanent resident of Japan.
2.A foreign national who is not currently residing in Japan.
3.Individuals with the linguistic skills required to take classes and receive research guidance in English.
Master’s course applicants
4.Those who have completed, or are expected to complete, 16 years of school education by September 30 of the enrolment year, upon completion of their Bachelor's or equivalent degree.*
Doctoral course applicants
5.Those who have completed, or are expected to complete, 18 years of school education by September 30 of the enrolment year, upon completion of their Master's or equivalent degree.*
* Certified proof must be provided if the educational curriculum in the applicant's country requires less than 16 years for completion of a Bachelor's degree, or less than 18 years for completion of a Master's degree, or their equivalents.
MEXT Scholarship Applicants
- Individuals below the age of 35 as of April 1st of the enrolment year.
- Individuals with the nationality of a country with diplomatic relations with the Japanese government.
ADB-Japan Scholarship Applicants.
- Individuals below the age of 36 as of April 1st of the enrolm

FINANCIAL INFORMATION
Tuition Fee
In the Department of Civil Engineering, all students are required to be full-time degree students from the time of their enrolment until the completion of their degree program. Accordingly, the full rate of tuition will be charged to all students. Please refer to the University of Tokyo website to see the tuition rates and fees.
http://www.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/prospective-students/tuition_fees.html
Maintenance and living expenses
Along with the University fees mentioned at the link above, a prospective student must consider the living expenses to be incurred while living in Tokyo. This includes expenses such as insurance (medical, housing, accident), housing and rent, food, clothing, expenses related to study (books, copies, stationaries, etc.), commuting and transportation fees, air fare costs for arriving in Japan and returning to ones’ home country, and so on. Obviously, if family members must also be supported during your stay in Tokyo, these costs will rise.
Due to the relatively high living costs of Tokyo, the Department of Civil Engineering estimates that a standard monthly budget for a student in a degree program should be at least 150,000 Japanese Yen (or more). Therefore, the ideal amount of total resources (i.e., educational fees and living expenses) that a single student must cover, at a minimum, across the number of years required to complete a degree program are as follows:
Doctoral Course
Master's Course
Accordingly, a “Full-Scholarship” should cover:
- Full tuition fee
- Admission fee
- Examination fee
- A monthly subsistence and housing allowance
- A round-trip air ticket
- Medical insurance

SPECIAL ADMISSION
Application Guidelines only for
Integrated Master/PhD Program
Doctoral Course
This is a special program which offers non-Japanese students an opportunity to work toward a Doctor degree.
Integrated Master/PhD Program has been set up for students who wish to obtain a Doctor degree after Master's program. Program students are given special opportunities to focus on cutting-edge research and they will receive preferential treatment in the process of acquiring scholarships. Program students will be required to sit a Qualifying Examination (QE) one year after admission. The QE will determine which students will be offered a place on the Integrated Master/PhD Program as doctoral students (Students need procedures to step up to the doctoral program). The applicants are advised to discuss further details, such as application timelines and required documents, with expected supervisors before applying.
Doctoral Course is a course that aims for students who have completed a master's course to obtain a Doctor degree.
These application guidelines include all of the requirements for students applying for the Master's Course (Integrated Master/PhD Program) and Doctoral Course of Special Admission and International Graduate Program in Chemistry and Biotechnology. Applicants must read the specific application guidelines found below before applying.
The applicants must have foreign citizenship and an undergraduate certificate (or expect to receive it by March 31st, 2027) from an overseas university (Please read the âGuidelines for Applicantsâ carefully). The applicants should take a guidance from the expected supervisor before applying and receive âConfirmation Noticeâ with the supervisorâs signature.
For April 2027 Entry (Taking an onsite exam in Tokyo)
This special admission examination is for applicants without Japanese citizenship applying for the Integrated Master/PhD Program and Doctoral Course. Applicants must also have received a masterâs (for Doctoral Course) or bachelor's (for Integrated Master/PhD program) degree certificate (or are expecting to graduate by March 31, 2027) from an overseas university.
Please note:
Applicants must contact their expected supervisors before applying.
See also
Necessary documents specific to the Department of Chemistry and Biotechnology
Please check our website for the latest information.

Examination Date: January 20th, 2027

(2) Oral Examination for 20 minutes: General questions concerning chemistry and biotechnology including your research for bachelorâs thesis.
Examination Date: January 20th, 2027
Examination Time: To be announced
Location: To be announced
During the oral examination, applicants will be asked to answer general questions in the field of chemistry and/or biotechnology, and to describe their current or past research experiences, such as a bachelor's thesis project. Applicants who received an undergraduate degree without a research component (lab-based or library-based) will also be considered.
